Therefore, after the multiplication and preparation, its **Tincture** <note>The transformative power or "dye" of the Stone.</note> is increased.

But **for the Sun** <note>original: *ad Solem*; referring to the "Red Work" or the creation of gold, symbolized by the Sun.</note>, one must cook the tincture longer by the fire until it becomes red. For the redness is nothing other than a perfection of the **digestions** <note>original: *Dauungen*; the prolonged heating of a substance at a steady temperature to mature it.</note>, and thus likewise becomes an **Elixir** for the red and the white: so that for every red thing you do not apply white, but rather the white to the white, and the red to the red, as **the Philosopher** <note>original: *Philosophus*; likely referring to Hermes Trismegistus or Aristotle, common authorities in alchemical texts.</note> says: But his meaning is not that you place anything in it by hand, neither powder nor water, nor any physical thing. Rather, it means that you add its own tincture to it, as is written hereafter.

Now when the intense **ignition** <note>original: *Entzündung*; referring to the increase of heat in the furnace.</note> occurs, the contents move about within the vessel like boiling water; thus the body is as much above as it is below. For Nature allows no empty space to exist between them, and this is its <term>distillation</term> <note>The process of purifying a substance by heating it into vapor and then condensing it.</note> and <term>sublimation</term> <note>In alchemy, the "lifting" of a material essence from its coarse base.</note>.

However, some believe that the <term>Elixir</term> <note>The "Philosopher's Stone" in its liquid or medicinal form.</note> must be sublimated [externally], yet they do not possess the mastery of the **philosophers** or understand their meaning. Instead, after long waiting and constant cooking in the fire, the substance becomes fixed, and that is its <term>calcination</term> <note>original: *Calcinatio*; reducing a solid to a fine powder through heat.</note> and <term>coagulation</term> <note>original: *Coagulatio*; the thickening or solidifying of the liquid into a stable form.</note>. Thus, it appropriately turns white in the dry stage, yellow in the white stage, and red in the yellow stage, as was said before. 

But between the black and the white colors, every color that a man can imagine will appear. These, however, are not to be regarded, as they are not permanent, until the true Whiteness arrives. This is the truest <term>Elixir</term>, composed of transparent things. For once it becomes an <term>Elixir</term>, it is visible, yet even more so—having the color of a pure mirror-glass, heavenly—and it becomes gentle in the trials of men through the aforementioned White.

And so the work ends in three times forty days, with each stage lasting forty days. In the first forty days, the **Man** rules, and his <term>seed</term> <note>original: *Sperma*; refers to the "Sulfur" or active masculine principle in the chemical marriage.</note> unites with the **Woman** <note>The "Mercury" or passive feminine principle.</note> so that they shall never again be parted from one another for all eternity. In the second forty days, it is formed and receives the **Soul** and the **Spirit**,

...and the **Tincture** <note>The "soul" or coloring principle of the Stone.</note>, and it becomes alive. In the third forty days <note>Completing a traditional 120-day cycle often cited in alchemical literature.</note>, the pure fruit is born, to which nothing else is equal. It becomes fusible and liquid, and through its fiery spirit, it no longer fears the fire; rather, the fire rests within it kindly. Thus, it shall rule over all the treasures of the entire world. 

For it keeps bodies healthy and heals all infirmities <note>original: *Siech-Tagen*; literally "sick-days" or chronic illnesses.</note>, whether they be of a hot or cold nature <note>A reference to the Galenic theory of humors, where diseases were classified by their perceived temperature.</note>. Use this for the honor of God and the benefit of your neighbor; otherwise, God will not grant it to you. For He has entirely taken it away and hidden it from the haughty and the arrogant, but has opened it to those lovers of the art who keep it secret and concealed. Praise and honor be to God on the highest throne, etc.

Finally and in conclusion, for the sake of the gracious reader <note>original: *G. L.*, likely standing for *Günstiger Leser*.</note>, I have also wished to append the

### **Universal Perfection**

which little treatise was published in the year 1619 and is now quite rare, in order to further prove the possibility of this noble art, because there is much good within it. Whoever is wise can omit the common **process of amalgamation** <term>Amalgamation: The metallurgical process of bonding mercury with another metal, such as gold or silver. In alchemy, this is often a metaphor for the union of spirit and body.</term> from it, or must understand it quite differently; for it was placed therein merely to obscure the entire matter, so that one should not so easily learn to distinguish the good from the false. 

For if this process of amalgamation with common, false **quicksilver** or **mercury** <note>original: *Argento vivo* or *Mercurio*</note> (which is described therein clearly and distinctly enough) were true, then the **Philosopher's Stone** <note>original: *L. P.*, abbreviation for *Lapis Philosophorum*.</note> would have long ago become more common than it is now. I know, however, that so many have tried this, and the art has always failed, and nothing has come of it. 

Notwithstanding this, even if someone has cleared this **obstacle** <note>original: *Obstaculum*</note> aside, he will still have enough to do before he brings the entire process to its conclusion. Here, too, the words of **Virgil** in the sixth book of the *Aeneid* will apply:

> The golden branch itself will follow willingly and easily, if the fates call you; otherwise, you will not be able to conquer it by any strength, nor tear it away with hard iron. <note>original Latin: *Aureus ipse volens ramus facilisque sequetur, Si te fata vocant: aliter non viribus ullis Vincere, nec duro poteris convellere ferro.*</note>

Therefore, first of all, keep God before your eyes, live in His fear, pray diligently, and be merciful toward your neighbor; thus God will grant you grace for this art. For this reason, mark well what is necessary for you to know:

Primarily, what there is in the earth among the minerals of the seven main metals, both the physical and the spiritual. Namely: Mercury <note>original: *Mercurius*</note>, that is <note>original: *h. e.*, abbreviation for the Latin *hoc est*.</note>, quicksilver (which is spiritual).

The physical ones, however, are Saturn <term>Saturn: The alchemical name for Lead.</term>, Jupiter <term>Jupiter: The alchemical name for Tin.</term>, Mars <term>Mars: The alchemical name for Iron.</term>, Sol <term>Sol: The Latin name for the Sun, representing Gold.</term>, Venus <term>Venus: The alchemical name for Copper.</term>, and Luna <term>Luna: The Latin name for the Moon, representing Silver.</term>; these are held and esteemed by natural philosophers <term>Physici: A term for those who study the laws of nature, including physicians and alchemists.</term> as the princes and chief planets of the minerals of the earth.

Therefore, just as the seven main planets in the heavens above have their specific effect and nature distinct from the other stars in the sky, so too do the seven main metals of the earth have their specific nature and effect above other minerals.

Therefore, as mentioned, the seven main metals are named and designated by the names of the planets; and those which exist in their substances have their nature, growth, and origin from constant quicksilver <note>original: *Argento vivo*</note> and magnesia <term>Magnesia: In early alchemy, this was not modern magnesium, but a term for a "primary matter" or a specific mineral substance believed to contain the "seed" of metals.</term> and incombustible sulfur <note>original: *Schwefel*</note>, which is constant in fire and not volatile, nor smoking away like common sulfur and mercury do, before the mi-

...[al]le witness and say: Without these three stones, you accomplish nothing.

The second is **Luna** <note>Luna is the traditional alchemical name for Silver, representing the cool, feminine principle.</note>, the woman—that is, the steadfastly cooked Mercury which has taken a permanent body unto itself and become silver.

...[if it is not] moistened <note>This continues the thought from the previous page: if the matter is not given "drink" (liquid mercury) beyond the fourth day.</note> beyond the 4th day, it burns within itself and dies; such is also the nature of this work.

Furthermore, you should take care that in your work of the First Matter <note>original: *primæ Materiæ*</note>, when you perform the dissolution, you allow no worldly fire nor the light of the sun to enter or shine upon it, for such a thing is entirely destructive. Its blossoms and flowers <note>original: *Flores und Blumen*; in alchemy, these colorful crystals or chemical "blooms" appearing on the surface of the matter indicate the progress of the Great Work.</note> would be harmed by it, and your work might ignite of its own accord, permeate, and turn to poison. For your work possesses a more wonderful power than any other, because it is a doubled Mercury <note>original: *Mercurius*; likely referring to "Sophic Mercury" or mercury that has been joined with its opposite principle.</note>, and at the foundation of this work, it is the Sulfur <note>original: *Sulphur*</note> that helps to bring it to completion.

Therefore, to prevent this danger, have a box made for yourself out of oak wood <note>original: *Eichen-Holz*</note> that is split in the middle, so that you can quickly pull it away from the glass <note>original: *Glaß*</note> and the ash <note>original: *Asche*</note>. This ash should also be burned from oak wood, and afterwards well-boiled, purified of dross <note>original: *geschleimet*; literally "de-slimed," a process of washing the ash to remove soluble impurities.</note>, and well-dried again. Place your work into this ash within the boxes, and set it so that it stands upright, so that you can see the matter <note>original: *Materia*</note>. The glass should sit deeper in the ash than the matter which is inside the glass, for the ash protects your work from internal elemental combustion, so that it suffers no harm.

### Report on How the Mercury Should Be Prepared

The Mercury <note>original: *Mercurius*; in alchemy, Mercury is not just the liquid metal but the "living" spirit or "feminine" principle necessary for the transformation of metals.</note> for your work is purified in two ways. The first is that you sublimate <note>To heat a substance until it becomes vapor and then solidifies again, leaving impurities behind.</note> the Mercury seven times through the helm <note>The "helm" or alembic head is the top part of a distilling apparatus where vapors condense.</note> by itself <note>original: *per se*</note>, so that it may leave behind its foreign and evil matter <note>original: *Materiam*</note>, and so that it may be strengthened through the driving and motion of the worldly fire, becoming more spiritual <note>In this context, "spiritual" refers to making the substance more volatile or refined into a gas-like state.</note> the longer it is treated, until it becomes powerful enough to perform your work.

The other way to purge <note>original: *purgiren*</note> the Mercury: take care that you can obtain such Mercury that has flowed together in the earth itself, which is called Virgin Mercury <note>original: *Jungfer-Quecksilber*; mercury found in its pure liquid state in nature, considered superior to mercury extracted from ore by smelting.</note>. Wash the same very well with vinegar and salt, press it through a buckskin <note>original: *Bock-Fell*; used as a fine filter to remove physical dross.</note>, and then you have purged the Mercury well. You need not fear any fraud, such as suffering harm from foreign impurities, for Mercury is often adulterated with lead or bismuth <note>original: *Wißmuth*</note>.

Therefore, see that you obtain digested <note>original: *digerirten*; mercury that has been matured or "ripened" through gentle heat.</note> Mercury, or Hungarian Mercury, which would be even better, though not quite so much depends upon it.

Several natural philosophers <note>original: *Physici*</note> have sublimated and extracted the Mercury from gold-pyrite <note>original: *Gold-Kieß*; a mineral, often iron pyrite, believed by alchemists to contain the "seed" of gold.</note>, so that the Mercury has been strengthened with its spirit, whereby it has been able to dissolve the bodies <note>original: *Corpora*; the solid metals or substances intended for transmutation.</note> all the sooner and make them like unto itself.

<summary>This page describes two primary methods for purifying Mercury: repeated sublimation to refine its "spiritual" power, or the filtration of naturally occurring "Virgin Mercury." It warns against the common practice of adulterating mercury with lead or bismuth.</summary>
<keywords>Mercury, sublimation, Virgin Mercury, purification, gold-pyrite, alchemy, bismuth, distillation</keywords>

### How the Amalgam is to be Made.

The amalgam <note>A mixture of mercury with another metal.</note> for this work must be prepared in this way: place into a dissolving dish gold and silver <note>original: *Solem und Lunam*</note> beaten into thin sheets, two *Loth* <note>An old German unit of weight, approximately 15 to 17 grams.</note> of each, counted as one part; against this, take three parts—that is, three times as much mercury. Rub or grind these well together; this is the Amalgam of the Sages for your work, etc.

## Account of How One Should Prepare the Mercury.

The Mercury <note>The alchemical name for quicksilver, representing the fluid, feminine principle in the "Great Work."</note> for your work is purified in two ways. The first is that you sublimate <note>To heat a substance until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again, a process used here to refine it.</note> the Mercury seven times over the helm <note>The "helm" is the head of a distilling apparatus (alembic) where vapors condense.</note> on its own <note>original: *per se*</note>, so that it may leave behind its foreign and evil matter <note>original: *Materiam*</note>, and so that it may be strengthened by the driving and motion of the worldly fire—becoming increasingly spiritual <note>In alchemy, "spiritual" refers to a substance being refined into a volatile or gaseous state.</note> until it is powerful enough to perform your work.

The other way to purge the Mercury: take care that you obtain such Mercury as has gathered together within the earth itself, which is called **Virgin Mercury** <note>original: *Jungfer-Quecksilber*; naturally occurring mercury found in its pure liquid state in mines, highly prized by alchemists.</note>. Wash this very well with vinegar and salt, and press it through a buckskin <note>original: *Bock-Fell*; used as a fine filter to remove solid impurities from liquid mercury.</note>. In this way, you have purged the Mercury well and need not fear any deception or that you might suffer harm from foreign impurities, for Mercury is often adulterated with lead or bismuth.

Therefore, see that you obtain **digested** <note>original: *digerirten*; referring to a substance that has been subjected to gentle, continuous heat to mature it.</note> Mercury, or Hungarian Mercury, which would be even better; however, not everything depends on this.

Several physicists <note>original: *Physici*; in this context, natural philosophers or alchemists.</note> have sublimated and extracted the Mercury from **gold pyrites** <note>original: *Gold-Kieß*; an ore, likely iron pyrites containing traces of gold.</note>, so that the Mercury was strengthened with its spirit, enabling it to dissolve the bodies <note>original: *Corpora*; the solid metals, such as gold or silver, that the mercury must break down.</note> all the sooner and make them equal to itself.

<summary>This page outlines two primary methods for purifying mercury: repeated distillation to "spiritualize" it and the use of naturally occurring "Virgin Mercury" filtered through leather. It warns against common metal adulterants like lead and emphasizes the need for a "strong" mercury capable of dissolving solid metals.</summary>

### Now follows the Fixation and Dissolution of the Body:

In the name of the holy and undivided Trinity, take 2 loth <note>A historical German unit of weight; 2 loth is approximately 1 ounce or 30 grams.</note> of the aforementioned thin-beaten Gold, and also 2 loth of thin-beaten fine Silver. This is so that one can better preserve the spirits <note>original: *Spiritus*</note> or Mercury when they are dissolved and subsequently placed into the worldly fire. For even if the spirits rise from the heat, the length of the glass neck will stop them, and they must sink back down and be consumed within the matter itself.

...[place it in a] location, so that it takes no damage from external moisture, and cover the glass with diligence, so that the sun's glare, wind, or rain does not touch it or inflict harm; or have a cover made over it so that no air reaches it. Let it stand for seven full days; thereafter, on the eighth day, renew its warmth and refresh its spirit <note>original: *Muth*; literally "courage" or "mood," but in alchemical contexts refers to the internal vitality or "spirit" of the substance</note>—that is, the elemental spiritual fire. Add to it two more loth <note>A historical German unit of weight; 2 loth is approximately 1 ounce or 30 grams.</note> of the purified mercury <note>original: *Mercurii*</note>, and let it stand again for four days. On the fifth day, add another two loth of the aforementioned mercury, and so on and so forth, until 36 loth of mercury have been added to the matter. Thus, in total, there are the two loth of gold and two loth of silver, and the [initial] twelve loth of mercury, after which two loth are added again every fifth day. Continue to augment it in this way until 48 loth of mercury have been added to the two loth of gold and two loth of silver. All this occurs within 90 days, counting from the first imbibition <term>Eintränkung|The process of 'feeding' or saturating a solid substance with a liquid (mercury in this case) to soften and dissolve it</term> on the seventh day.

When you have thus, through God's gracious blessing, subsequently augmented or renewed the work on the fifth day, and it is boiled through with its spirit in its own fire, which is hidden within them, then the bodies dissolve into their own nature and blossom. They become one single thing, which they were originally from the beginning; namely, it becomes their First Matter <term>Prima Materia|The original, undifferentiated substance from which all metals were thought to originate</term>—

<meta>This page continues the specific instructions for the "imbibition" or saturation process described on the previous page. It explains the results of the rhythmic soaking and the physical growth of the matter as it transitions through the stage of blackness.</meta>

-> ❁ ) 0 ( ❁ <-

A **thin film** <note>original: *Tüchlein*; literally "little cloth," likely referring to a skin or film forming on the surface of the matter.</note> has come out of the glass, which is perhaps 50 times as large as the **body** <note>original: *Cörper*</note> was; it is all together imbibed into the **blackness** <note>original: *Schwärze*</note>. After each **application** <note>original: *Anfechtung*; here referring to the "attack" or addition of the liquid to the solid matter.</note>, always let it stand for two days; thus the **body** <term>Body: The solid, material part of the alchemical substance, often seen as the "base" or vessel for the spirit.</term> <note>original: *Corpus*</note> is made and enlarged by every **imbibition** <term>Imbibition: The process of repeatedly adding liquid (usually mercury) to a solid until it is fully saturated.</term>, and the **mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius*</note> dissolves from time to time into the blackness, and with it becomes a black **ash**, becoming a single thing and essence together with the **body**, and through this **cooking** <note>original: *Kochung*</note> it is purified, whitened, and made subtle.

Therefore, because the **body** is enlarged and increased after every **imbibition**, you must also always add more and more with each **refreshment**, yet always in such weight that the **dryness** is greater, so that it can overcome the **moisture**. Thus you can also, from one **soaking** <note>original: *Eintränkung*</note> to the next, strengthen the **worldly fire** <note>original: *weltlichen Feuer*; the external heat applied by the furnace, as opposed to the internal "natural" or "secret" fire of the materials.</note> from time to time, always gradually; for it can never endure the heat for long [all at once], just as the food and drink is always increased and multiplied—as may be seen in the example of a young **animal**; the larger it grows, the more it can endure. In the beginning, soon after birth, it takes in very little milk and nourishment, but the larger it becomes, the more drink and nourishment it must have; so it is also with our **work**.

Again, a black <term>body</term> <note>original: *Corpus*</note> will settle on top, and the matter below is oily, for which reason this is called <term>Oil of Sulphur</term> <note>original: *Oleum Sulphuris*; a term for the concentrated "soul" or active principle of the matter.</note>. 

When this has stood thus for ten days without <term>worldly fire</term> <note>original: *weltlich Feuer*; physical heat from a furnace or flame, as opposed to the "innate" or "secret" heat of the substances themselves.</note>, and you do not find that it begins to turn to <term>blackness</term> <note>original: *Schwärze*; the *nigredo* stage, representing the symbolic death and purification of the matter.</note> and dissolve, then the <term>elemental fire</term> <note>original: *elementische Feuer*</note> is too strong for it and the moisture too weak, so that it cannot overcome the dryness. In that case, you must come to the aid of the moisture with our <term>mastery</term> <note>original: *Meisterschaft*; the skilled application of alchemical principles.</note>, and add more moisture of the mercury to your work, and let it stand thus in the cold ash until the moisture overcomes and conquers the dryness, and your work is completely and utterly dissolved, and a blackness will settle on top; this you [will achieve] in 30 or 40 days without worldly fire

...conquer the dryness, for the <term>blackness</term> <note>original: *Schwärz*; referring to the *nigredo* stage, the initial phase of decomposition in the alchemical Great Work.</note> must always be more dominant in its work than the water and moisture you add. Because the <term>body</term> <note>original: *Corpus*; the solid physical substance being worked upon.</note> is now strengthened and has grown, the fire can also be increased. You can complete such a <term>re-coagulation</term> <note>original: *Eincoagulirung*.</note> in 35 or 40 days; it does not require as much time now as it did in the first operation. This is because the dryness of the <term>matter</term> <note>original: *Materia*.</note> and the intensity of the fire are both greater than they were during the first coagulation.

When you have once again dried and increased your body as instructed, give it a fairly strong worldly fire and burn it thoroughly so that it becomes beautifully white. The more you burn it, the whiter it becomes; and this stage is called the **White Swan** <note>original: *weisse Schwan*; a classic alchemical symbol for the *albedo* or "whitening" stage, representing the purification of the matter.</note> by the <term>natural philosophers</term> <note>original: *Physicis*; early scientists and alchemists who studied the laws of nature.</note>.

# Now follows the **Augmentation** and great **increase** to **tincture** <note>original: *tingiren*; the act of applying a "tincture" to transform or color a base metal into a noble one.</note> in Red and White.

The ignorant alchemists cannot find or understand much from the **Physicists** <note>original: *Physicis*; in the 17th century, this referred to natural philosophers or those who studied the laws of nature, rather than modern physics.</note> and their books in their foolish thoughts, such that they would know that our work has only one **entrance** <note>original: *Ingreß*; the point at which the alchemical agent penetrates the metal.</note> or **tincture**. For they claim out of sheer ignorance that for the **white tincture**, only the white **body** <note>original: *Corpus*; the physical substance of the metal.</note>—which is **Luna** <note>original: *Luna*; the alchemical name for silver.</note>—must be used, but for the **red tincture**, only gold must come and no silver. But that is not true; rather, it is false and fabricated. Hear what the **Physicists** <note>original: *Physici*.</note> say and speak of this: "What body has become gold, that was not first silver, and afterward became gold?" <note>original: *Welch Corpus ist Gold worden, das nicht zuvor Silber gewesen...*</note> They have read the books of the **Physicists** <note>original: *Physicorum*; the masters of natural philosophy.</note> well and heard their voices well, but they say afterward that they did not understand it.

Therefore, know that in our work there is only one **government** <note>original: *Regierung*; the "regimen" or controlled sequence of heating and chemical stages.</note> in the beginning, middle, and end. There is only one tincture, one root, which bears a fruitful tree that spreads out into two branches, one of which brings or carries white fruit and the other red; yet they are not two trees, but only one tree, as you can see such things on natural trees with visible

<summary>This page critiques amateur alchemists for believing that the Red and White elixirs require entirely different starting materials, arguing instead that all gold begins as silver and that the entire Great Work stems from a single "root" or process.</summary>
<keywords>Augmentation, Tincture, Physicists, Luna, Silver, Gold, Regimen, Alchemical Tree</keywords>

...[you] see with your own eyes that both red and white apples are planted and grafted onto a single tree; so it is also with our work: it is but one art and one work, yet it bears two kinds of fruit, namely the white and the red.

Furthermore, in their unwise senses and thoughts, they do not consider the properties of Nature: that in a natural way, through the cohabitation of the man and through the power of his **seed** <note>original: *Spermatis*; here referring to the reproductive essence or "sperm" in a pre-modern biological sense.</note> with the woman, her nature might bear sons and daughters—both male and female genders. This could not happen at all if the man were alone or the woman were alone; rather, through the mixing of them both, they afterwards bear many sons and daughters.

For I tell you in the highest truth that it happens with our work just as it does with man and woman: for **Gold** is the man, **Silver** is the woman, and **Mercury** <term>Mercurius; in alchemy, Mercury represents the fluid, transformative principle and the "seed" or "spirit" that connects all metals.</term> is the desirable seed of the man and woman, which is the origin of all metals. From this, you must clearly understand that our art is only one work that brings forth two kinds of fruit.

Now, observe further with all diligence the division of the **Elixir**. The aforementioned white **Body** <note>original: *Corpus*; the physical substance resulting from the previous stages of purification.</note> or **Elixir** is here divided into two parts: one for the Red, and one for the

<summary>The author uses biological and botanical analogies—grafting fruit trees and human reproduction—to explain that the alchemical process is a single path that yields two distinct results: the White and Red Elixirs. It identifies Gold as the masculine principle, Silver as the feminine, and Mercury as the mediating "seed" of all metals.</summary>

Preserve this well, and keep it with diligence, because this elixir or white **Body** <note>original: *Corpus*; the physical substance resulting from the previous stages of purification.</note> is powerful and great in its virtue to perform wonders, and it can **tinge** <term>tinge; to transform or "dye" a base metal into a noble one through alchemical projection.</term> many thousand parts into permanent silver. Stir it well and quickly, and it will soon **coagulate** <term>coagulate; the process where a liquid or vaporous substance becomes solid or fixed.</term> and bring it under its power into its own essence; this too is an Elixir.

Thirdly, take once again a hundred parts of **purified Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurii purgati*</note>, place it in a fire, and take one part of the elixir that has been **tinged** <term>tinge; to transform the nature of a base metal into a noble one through the alchemical "dye" or elixir.</term> twice. ### Concerning the Medicine of this Universal.

These two **elixirs**, white and red—understand that before they are increased with a hundred parts of **mercury** <note>original: *Mercurii*</note> and while they are still in their original state—possess such power and virtue that they heal all illnesses and restore health. This is attested to by **Arnoldus** <note>Likely Arnold of Villanova (c. 1240–1311), a celebrated physician and alchemist whose works bridged the gap between medicine and alchemy.</note>, who says: Our **Stone** <term>Stone; the Philosopher's Stone, the crowning achievement of alchemy, believed to be a "universal" cure-all for both metals and the human body.</term> has the power to heal sickness beyond all the **medicine of the physicians** <note>original: *Medicin der Medicorum*</note>, for it makes people joyful, prevents the blood from putrefying, and does not allow the "flame" <note>Likely referring to fever or inflammation.</note> to prevail. It allows neither **melancholy** <note>original: *Melancholiam*; according to the ancient theory of humors, an excess of "black bile" thought to cause depression and sluggishness.</note> nor **choler** <note>original: *Choleram*; an excess of "yellow bile" associated with anger, heat, and digestive issues.</note> to rule the body; indeed, it strengthens all limbs very greatly and powerfully and does not let them decay. It heals all weaknesses, and even if an illness had lasted an entire year and no one could cure it, if you give the patient a mustard-seed's weight of this **white elixir** to drink in wine, he will become healthy in twelve days;
